# SOURCES — where kotodama learns

The lane's curated reading list and the standing record of its taste:
what each source teaches, how far to trust it for which scope, and when to
look again. Shape and rules: fleet contract `SOURCES-CONTRACT-v1`
(kaika, 2026-10-19). Claim admission stays in `QUALITY.md`; the research
method stays chousa's. Every row was opened in the round that wrote it —
the round lesson's `## Gate` (`lessons/round-49.md` for S001–S047, `lessons/round-50.md` for S048–S070, and from S071 onward the lesson of the round dated in the row's `opened` cell and search-log note) holds
each row's URL, access date and verbatim quote. Append-only: only
`status`, `recheck` and `used-by` change in place, each change logged
old→new in a round lesson; a correction is a new row whose `teaches`
opens `supersedes S0NN —`.

Tiers are scoped to the row's `scope`, never global. **A** — primary or
authoritative for that scope (spec, official docs, source code, original
paper, maintainer statement, the lane's own re-runnable gate output).
**B** — strong secondary (expert write-up with reproducible detail, survey,
textbook). **C** — lead only, never sole support for an admitted entry.
`community` is never A. Tier is curation, not truth. Status: `queued`
(opened, worth mining) · `mined` (cited by the paths in `used-by`) ·
`rejected` (kept with reason and flip — a rejection is evidence of taste).

Census (from the lane root): the seven commands in the contract's
`## Census` block; `python3 reports/round-49/run-validation.py` runs them.

## Paths

- Typology evidence chain — S001 → S002 → S003 → S004: name the languoid in
  Glottolog first (it fixes identity, family and the glottocode every other
  dataset keys on), then take feature proportions from WALS, then check segment
  plausibility against PHOIBLE inventories, then Grambank for grammatical design
  space beyond what WALS covers.
- Kinship and society grounding — S007 → S005: read D-PLACE codings for the
  society's stated regime first (tagged by date and source), then Kinbank for the
  actual term inventories and its worked null-result caution before asserting
  any marriage–terminology coupling.
- Lexical history design — S006 → S011 → S010: mine DatSemShift for attested
  meaning-change directions, sanity-check sound-change steps against the Index
  Diachronica's cited sources (never the index alone), then replay the whole
  staged history deterministically in Lexurgy.
- Conlang method — S008 → S009 → S012: learn the craft order from the LCK, then
  consult CALS only as a census of what other conlangers choose (community tier),
  then ConlangCrafter to see what staged LLM pipelines now automate and where
  their evaluation is still thin.
- Motif-grid grounding — S013 → S014 → S015: anchor motif identities in the
  digital TMI (verifying load-bearing IDs against print), read MOMFER for the
  index's measured skews and double-entries before drawing distribution
  conclusions, and use the CSV for executable motif-ID existence checks.
- folktale index practice — S018 → S016 → S017: see what phylogenetic inference can and cannot date before learning the current type index, then add the areal catalogue that corrects the index's Eurocentric bins.
- belief legends — S019 → S020: the live archive corpus first, then the state of the art in automated indexing built on a comparable corpus (Arabian Nights + El-Shamy).
- period and polity assertion — S022 → S023: the data model that makes a period citable (name, bounds, region, source) before the spatial dataset the periods hang on; note Cliopatria does not encode border uncertainty.
- costume cataloguing — S024 → S025 → S026: the museum naming method that keeps function separate from the garment name, then the controlled term pool with usage flags, then the dated historical lexis for medieval Britain.
- iconography — S027 → S025: the subject index first, then the AAT boundary note — AAT deliberately excludes iconographic subjects, so the two vocabularies divide the work.
- LLM-assisted narrative — S021 → S020: why generated stories converge on a mean narrative before trusting automated motif indexing on generated or annotated corpora.
- women's hunting — S041 → S031 → S033 → S032: archaeology first (one securely dated burial and a meta-analysis), then the ethnographic tally, then the authors' own correction, then the critique — read its body before repeating any number.
- mobility and dependence — S040 → S034 → S042: Binford's two settlement strategies, Kelly's variability review that turns them into a spectrum, then the coded dependence distribution that bounds what a gathering culture can be.
- sharing and the romantic frames — S035 → S038 → S037 → S036: the storage-and-equality typology, the affluence thesis, the noble-savage critique, then the Kalahari debate on foragers in history — each a frame to calibrate, none a description.
- optimality — S039 → S034: the model family first, then where it explains forager variation and where culture outruns it.
- signed-language design — S071 → S096 → S133 → S095 → S087 → S100 → S118: Stokoe's parameters first, then the minimal-pair and two-hand-condition modules that report Battison (S094 is rejected: QUALITY criterion 10), then the Prosodic Model overview, then the emerging-language typology, then the one-generation grammar evidence, and the utopia critique before writing any shared-signing village.

## Search log

**Linguistics, typology, conlang and motif leads (rows S001–S015).** Question: which open typology, conlang-craft and motif-index sources can ground this lane's language and narrative work, and at what trust tier? Where searched: the fifteen lead URLs in the wave-1 ART dossier (rows S001–S015), each fetched and read directly on 2026-10-19 — no search engine involved; this is a dossier sweep, not a query sweep. Identified 15, screened 15, included 15 rows (13 carrying content, plus 2 community-tier census rows kept deliberately at C), excluded 0, rejected 0. Dossier claim corrections on reopening: (a) D-PLACE used-by shrank from the dossier's listed set — ancestral-puebloan.md and persia-sasanian.md match the substring 'd-place' only inside unrelated words, not as citations; (b) three draft `watch` statuses normalized to `queued` per the contract enum; (c) LCK gained a verified citing entry beyond the draft list (semantic-fields). Integrator corrections before landing (critic findings): a citation of a site's bibliographic reference record is not a citation of the catalogue or dataset a row scopes — so `hopi.md`, `ottoman.md`, `costume-language-coupling-table.md` (Glottolog references) left S001 `used-by`; `china-qing.md`, `malagasy-madagascar.md` (WALS refdb) left S002; `siam-thai.md` (a Grambank source record) left S004, which drops to `queued`; `mesoamerica-primary-sources.md` (a D-PLACE source record; a Paris-Codex "Thompson" attribution) left S007 and S013. Stop rationale: every dossier lead in the assigned band was opened; the remaining linguistics leads are listed under Leads.

**Folktale, legend, period, costume and iconography leads (rows S016–S030).** Re-opening sweep, 2026-10-19. Question: do ART dossier draft rows S016–S030 hold up as SOURCES.md rows, and does any existing kotodama knowledge entry cite the source? Where searched: ART dossier `kotodama-art.md` §3 draft table for the 15 URLs; `grep -rlnE` over `corpus/knowledge` for citation evidence. Identified 15 leads; screened 15 by opening each URL; included 12 rows (2 mined, 10 queued); 3 kept rejected after re-opening confirmed the rejection grounds. Excluded from rows: nothing beyond the three already-rejected items. Status corrections on re-verification: S016 and S018 mined (myth-consistency-gate.md cites the 2024 ATU edition notice URL; myth-folktale-genres.md cites da Silva & Tehrani 2016); S017 corrected to queued — corpus cites Berezkin's 2005/2010 journal articles, not the ruthenia.ru catalogue; S024 corrected to queued — corpus "ICOM" hits are ICOMOS and UNESCO, not the costume vocabulary; S025 corrected to queued — corpus getty.edu hits are publications and the Florentine Codex, not the AAT. Stop rationale: all 15 assigned leads resolved; per-lead depth limited to the anchor page needed to ground `teaches`.

**Hunting and foraging subsistence (rows S031–S043).** Question: which opened sources can ground a comparative hunting-and-foraging production register and its calibration bank (women's hunting, affluence, noble savage, living fossils, optimality, mobility)? Where searched: the entry seat's own reading list (PLOS ONE, DOI landing pages, publisher pages, D-PLACE), re-opened directly by the integrator on 2026-10-19 through the journal pages, the Crossref works API where publisher pages block or paywall (S032, S034, S035, S036, S040, S041), and one web search for popular coverage of the women-hunting debate. Identified 14 (12 cited by the entry, the ScienceDaily release, the Lacy and Ocobock primary it reports). Screened 13 by opening. Included 12 as mined rows; rejected 1 (S043, press release — no data or locators). Excluded from rows: Lacy and Ocobock 2023 (not opened — kept as a lead). Bibliographic-only rows (S032, S035, S036) are scoped to identity and the entry marks their content `(to-verify)`. Stop rationale: every source the entry cites is opened and scoped; further debate literature waits for a round that reads the S032 body.

**Pastoralism and animal husbandry (rows S048–S069).** Round 50, 2026-10-21. Question: which opened sources can ground a comparative pastoral production register — typology, species, herd management, products, wealth and exchange, tenure and commons, pastoral–sedentary relations, the herd's ritual ledger — and its calibration bank (stage ladder, pure nomad, cattle complex, tragedy of the commons, secondary-products horizon, Botai horses)? Where searched: FAO publications, PMC/Springer/Nature/SciELO/OpenEdition open-access papers, UNESCO ICH element records, the Swedish Sámi Parliament, ETCSL, AHD, D-PLACE, publisher pages; 22 build-wave source seats plus 13 section scribes opened candidates. Included 22 rows (A 16, B 6). Opened but not registered: Dyson-Hudson 1980 (identity only, no abstract), Herskovits 1926 (all routes 403; S053 carries the attribution), Galaty & Johnson 1990 (no scope text reachable), Khazanov 1994 publisher page (no interdependence statement), Barfield 1993 abstract record, FAO newsroom story and Knowledge Hub pages, Sametinget companion pages and statutes, Morello & Rizk 2022, Ostrom 1990 publisher record, Marett 1940 review, Goody & Tambiah via HRAF, Kozuh 2014 publisher summary, Hammurabi (Avalon), O'Hare 2025, Samec 2025, Bello-Bravo 2024 — leads, not rows, until a later round cites them. Failed: archive.org (ISP denial), Annual Reviews and Science publisher pages (403), Cambridge Core (429), live ETCSL host (timeout), UNESCO live pages (CAPTCHA; Wayback used and recorded).

**Sign languages and manual modality (rows S071–S124).** 2026-10-22. Question: which opened sources can ground a sign-language and manual-communication register for conlang and worldbuilding — register test, typology, community ecology, sublexical parameters, grammar in space, iconicity, emergence and families, alternate signing by hearing people, contact, codes and notation — and its calibration bank (one universal sign language, sign as pantomime, the village idyll, the critical period, counts of sign languages, terminology)? Where searched: journal and archive pages opened directly by 14 drafting seats and 2 attacking seats (PMC, PubMed, Europe PMC, ERIC and the Internet Archive, Glottolog, Ethnologue, ELAR, university repositories, the World Federation of the Deaf, the United Nations, the US national deaf association, the US National Archives, Cambridge, Gallaudet, De Gruyter and MIT publisher pages, SignWriting and the Hamburg HamNoSys pages, the Perseus Greek text); failed opens (HTTP 403 and 429, bot challenges) are recorded in the seat reports and not counted. Identified 88 drafted source rows; screened 88; included 54 rows (S071–S122 mined, S123 and S124 queued as leads; tier A 33, B 21). Merged 15 duplicate openings of one work into its single row. Replaced 13 encyclopedia pages (Wikipedia) by the primary rows that carry the same claims — a tertiary page is never sole support (QUALITY criterion 6). Excluded 6: a Wikimedia Commons film page and an archive finding aid (the National Archives record S111 carries the 1930 film), an Internet Archive bibliographic record, a third-party PDF copy and a World Federation of the Deaf page that duplicate registered rows, and a rights page no claim needs. Stop rationale: every factual cell of the entry rests on an opened tier-A or tier-B row or an in-corpus anchor, or carries `(to-verify)`.
